UnityEngine Object FindObjectsByType Retrieves a list of all loaded objects of Type type and sorts the results according to sortMode. Read time 4 minutes Last updated 2 months ago Definition Type: Method Namespace: UnityEngine Assembly: UnityEngine.CoreModule FindObjectsByType(Type, FindObjectsSortMode) Retrieves a list of all loaded objects of Type type and sorts the results according to sortMode . public static Object[] FindObjectsByType(Type type, FindObjectsSortMode sortMode) Parameters type Type The type of object to find. Must be a reference type derived from UnityEngine.Object. sortMode FindObjectsSortMode Whether and how to sort the returned array. Not sorting saves time, which can be significant if the search returns many objects. Returns Type Description Object[] An array of objects. Remarks FindObjectsByType doesn't return assets (for example meshes, textures, or prefabs). It also doesn't return objects that have HideFlags.DontSave set.Use Resources.FindObjectsOfTypeAll to avoid these limitations. You can't use Object.FindObjectsByType with interfaces directly. Instead, find all components of a base type and then filter the results by interface using LINQ's OfType . In the Editor, FindObjectsByType searches the Scene view by default. If you want to find an object in the Prefab stage, use the StageUtility APIs. using UnityEngine; // Ten GameObjects are created and have TextMesh and // CanvasRenderer components added. // When the game runs press the Space key to display the // number of TextMesh and CanvasRenderer components. public class ScriptExample : MonoBehaviour { private const int count = 10; void Start() { var gameObjects = new GameObject[count]; var expectedTextMeshObjects = new TextMesh[count]; var expectedCanvasObjects = new CanvasRenderer[count]; for (var i = 0; i < count; ++i) { gameObjects[i] = new GameObject(); expectedTextMeshObjects[i] = gameObjects[i].AddComponent<TextMesh>(); expectedCanvasObjects[i] = gameObjects[i].AddComponent<CanvasRenderer>(); } } void Update() { if (Input.GetKeyDown(KeyCode.Space)) { var foundCanvasObjects = FindObjectsByType<CanvasRenderer>(FindObjectsSortMode.None); var foundTextMeshObjects = FindObjectsByType(typeof(TextMesh), FindObjectsSortMode.None); Debug.Log(foundCanvasObjects + " : " + foundCanvasObjects.Length); Debug.Log(foundTextMeshObjects + " : " + foundTextMeshObjects.Length); } } } Additional Resources: Object.FindFirstObjectByType , Object.FindAnyObjectByType . FindObjectsByType(Type, FindObjectsInactive, FindObjectsSortMode) Retrieves a list of all loaded objects of Type type and sorts the results according to sortMode . public static Object[] FindObjectsByType(Type type, FindObjectsInactive findObjectsInactive, FindObjectsSortMode sortMode) Parameters type Type The type of object to find. Must be a reference type derived from UnityEngine.Object. findObjectsInactive FindObjectsInactive Whether to include components attached to inactive GameObjects. If you don't specify this parameter, this function doesn't include inactive objects in the results. sortMode FindObjectsSortMode Whether and how to sort the returned array. Not sorting saves time, which can be significant if the search returns many objects. Returns Type Description Object[] An array of objects. Remarks FindObjectsByType doesn't return assets (for example meshes, textures, or prefabs). It also doesn't return objects that have HideFlags.DontSave set.Use Resources.FindObjectsOfTypeAll to avoid these limitations. You can't use Object.FindObjectsByType with interfaces directly. Instead, find all components of a base type and then filter the results by interface using LINQ's OfType . In the Editor, FindObjectsByType searches the Scene view by default. If you want to find an object in the Prefab stage, use the StageUtility APIs. using UnityEngine; // Ten GameObjects are created and have TextMesh and // CanvasRenderer components added. // When the game runs press the Space key to display the // number of TextMesh and CanvasRenderer components. public class ScriptExample : MonoBehaviour { private const int count = 10; void Start() { var gameObjects = new GameObject[count]; var expectedTextMeshObjects = new TextMesh[count]; var expectedCanvasObjects = new CanvasRenderer[count]; for (var i = 0; i < count; ++i) { gameObjects[i] = new GameObject(); expectedTextMeshObjects[i] = gameObjects[i].AddComponent<TextMesh>(); expectedCanvasObjects[i] = gameObjects[i].AddComponent<CanvasRenderer>(); } } void Update() { if (Input.GetKeyDown(KeyCode.Space)) { var foundCanvasObjects = FindObjectsByType<CanvasRenderer>(FindObjectsSortMode.None); var foundTextMeshObjects = FindObjectsByType(typeof(TextMesh), FindObjectsSortMode.None); Debug.Log(foundCanvasObjects + " : " + foundCanvasObjects.Length); Debug.Log(foundTextMeshObjects + " : " + foundTextMeshObjects.Length); } } } Additional Resources: Object.FindFirstObjectByType , Object.FindAnyObjectByType . FindObjectsByType<T>(FindObjectsSortMode) Retrieves a list of all loaded objects of Type T. public static T[] FindObjectsByType<T>(FindObjectsSortMode sortMode) where T : Object Parameters sortMode FindObjectsSortMode Whether and how to sort the returned array. Not sorting saves time, which can be significant if the search returns many objects. Returns Type Description <T>[] The array of objects found matching the type specified. FindObjectsByType<T>(FindObjectsInactive, FindObjectsSortMode) Retrieves a list of all loaded objects of Type T. public static T[] FindObjectsByType<T>(FindObjectsInactive findObjectsInactive, FindObjectsSortMode sortMode) where T : Object Parameters findObjectsInactive FindObjectsInactive Whether to include components attached to inactive GameObjects. If you don't specify this parameter, this function doesn't include inactive objects in the results. sortMode FindObjectsSortMode Whether and how to sort the returned array. Not sorting saves time, which can be significant if the search returns many objects. Returns Type Description <T>[] The array of objects found matching the type specified.
